### Key Rating Drivers – Business Profile (F2)

**1. Elevated Insider Transaction Activity Reflects Potential Uncertainty**  
During 2022, Merck & Co., Inc. experienced a noteworthy volume of insider transactions, primarily in the form of awards and purchases, across multiple executives including named officers such as Mary Ellen Coe, Thomas H. Glocer, and Peter C. Wendell. While routine insider buying typically signals confidence, the frequency and clustering of transactions toward year-end—especially a concentration of awards—may indicate management’s efforts to retain executives amid evolving strategic challenges or uncertainty in the company’s near-term outlook. Such patterns can signal lack of clarity about future performance, potentially translating into increased credit risk due to management’s defensive compensation postures.

**2. Dilution Risk from Consistent Issuance of Phantom Stock and Awards**  
The repeated allocation of phantom stock and common stock awards to insiders suggests an ongoing reliance on equity-based compensation within the company’s remuneration framework. This approach, while standard in the pharmaceutical sector to incentivize performance, raises concerns about dilution for existing shareholders and bondholders, especially if sustained over longer periods. Excessive stock-based awards can erode financial flexibility and shareholder value, thereby increasing credit risk perceptions.

**3. Stable Yet Notably Dispersed Ownership Limits Strategic Alignment Risks**  
Analysis of insider ownership reveals a broadly distributed ownership base with no single executive or insider group exerting dominant control. Although dispersed ownership can mitigate risks stemming from shareholder activism, it also reduces direct alignment between management and significant shareholders, potentially diluting strategic cohesion. This fragmented internal ownership structure could delay decisive responses to market changes or competitive threats, inhibiting agile strategic execution critical in the pharmaceutical industry’s fast-evolving environment.

**4. Complex Partial Ownership Stakes in Numerous Biopharma Subsidiaries May Stretch Management Focus**  
Merck’s partial ownership (circa 10% stakes) of multiple biopharmaceutical entities such as BeOne Medicines Ltd., VYNE Therapeutics Inc., Organon & Co., and others, indicates a strategy leveraging minority positions in innovation-focused subsidiaries. While this may reduce direct capital exposure, it also introduces complexity in managing these portfolio companies and extracting value, especially as Merck lacks controlling influence. The potential for misalignment in priorities or underperformance at subsidiary levels could indirectly pressure the parent’s credit profile through reputational or financial linkages.

**5. Lack of Significant Insider Dispositions Suggests Maintenance Rather than Strengthening of Confidence**  
Throughout 2022, insider sales were minimal compared to award and purchase transactions, signaling no large-scale erosion of insider stakes. However, the absence of meaningful accumulation or concentration also suggests a cautious stance rather than overt confidence. This moderate approach may reflect management’s balanced but vigilant outlook amidst ongoing competitive and regulatory headwinds in the pharmaceutical industry.

---

Overall, Merck’s business profile is characterized by complex ownership layers, moderate insider confidence signaling, and compensation policies that raise potential dilution concerns. These factors, combined with management’s navigational challenges across its diverse biopharma investments, contribute to heightened operational risk that informs the company's credit risk positioning.
# V2 corrected
---------------------------------------------------
### Key Rating Drivers – Business Profile (F2)

**1. Elevated Insider Equity Awards Indicate Potential Dilution Risk**  
In 2022, Merck & Co., Inc. issued a significant volume of phantom stock and common stock awards to insiders, including multiple executives. While equity compensation is a standard element in the pharmaceutical sector to retain and motivate talent, the consistent issuance of awards presents a risk of shareholder dilution. This dilution can increase financial pressure on the company by potentially reducing earnings per share and shareholder value, which may weigh on credit considerations, especially if equity grants continue at a high pace without commensurate performance improvements.

**2. Dispersed Insider Ownership Limits Strategic Alignment and Control**  
Insider ownership at Merck remains broadly dispersed among executives without signs of significant concentration. While this mitigates risks of activist interventions or controlling conflicts, the lack of a dominant insider ownership group may lead to weaker alignment of strategic interests between management and investors. This dispersed control structure can constrain swift decision-making and sustained strategic focus, which is critical in managing competitive and regulatory challenges in the pharmaceutical sector.

**3. Partial Ownership Stakes in Multiple Minority Subsidiaries Increase Strategic Complexity**  
Merck holds approximately 10% ownership stakes in several biopharma subsidiaries, including Organon & Co., BeOne Medicines Ltd., VYNE Therapeutics Inc., and others. These minority positions limit Merck’s ability to exercise meaningful control or fully integrate these entities strategically. The complexity of managing multiple small stakes across various subsidiaries potentially dilutes focus and resources and exposes Merck indirectly to risks stemming from those subsidiaries’ performance, governance, and capital needs.

**4. Insider Transaction Pattern Shows Modest Buying with Minimal Dispositions**  
Throughout 2022, insider transactions were largely weighted toward stock awards and some purchases, with minimal sales activity. This pattern suggests a relatively stable insider confidence level without significant liquidity-driven disposals, but lacks evidence of substantial stake accumulation that would signal strong bullish sentiment. The transactional behavior reflects cautious confidence, which may indicate management’s prudent approach given external uncertainties rather than overt conviction in near-term prospects.

---

The analysis of Merck & Co., Inc.’s business profile highlights risks related to shareholder dilution from ongoing equity awards, a dispersed insider ownership structure potentially limiting strategic cohesion, and operational complexity arising from multiple minority stakes in biopharma subsidiaries. These factors present moderate business profile challenges that influence the company’s overall credit risk assessment.
# V3
---------------------------------------------------
### Key Rating Drivers – Operating Environment (F3)

**1. Geopolitical Instability and Inter-State Conflict Risks**  
Merck & Co., Inc.'s operating environment continues to be challenged by significant geopolitical tensions, notably the ongoing Russo-Ukrainian War that escalated in 2022. This conflict has caused considerable disruption across global supply chains and pharmaceutical markets, particularly in Europe and Eastern Europe where Merck has commercial interests. The risk of prolonged or expanded conflict could further interrupt manufacturing, distribution, and regulatory cooperation. Additionally, this instability increases geopolitical risk premiums and injects uncertainty into foreign exchange and trade policies, negatively impacting Merck’s international operations and revenue visibility.

**2. Heightened Regulatory and Political Transition Uncertainty in Key Markets**  
Frequent political power transfers and elections in the United States and other major markets where Merck operates introduce regulatory uncertainty. Major changes in U.S. presidential administrations in recent years (2016, 2020, and 2021 transitions) have periodically altered healthcare policy direction, drug pricing debates, and pharmaceutical reimbursement frameworks. Such political volatility complicates Merck’s long-term planning and pricing strategies, increasing the risk of sudden policy shifts that could adversely affect profitability and cash flow reliability. Moreover, regulatory environments remain fluid due to ongoing legislative initiatives targeting pharmaceutical patents and drug pricing reforms.

**3. Increasing Incidence of Health Crises and Pandemics**  
While healthcare companies can benefit from increased demand during health crises, the unpredictability and scope of recent pandemics pose operational and financial challenges. Merck has been repeatedly impacted by global health emergencies, including the SARS outbreak in China (2002-2004), the COVID-19 pandemic starting in 2020, and the 2022 monkeypox outbreak. These events have strained global healthcare systems and supply chains, triggered emergency regulatory approvals, and intensified competition in vaccine and antiviral markets. The constantly evolving nature of pandemics demands rapid R&D responses and capacity adjustments, straining resources and potentially affecting product pipelines and commercial execution.

**4. Social Unrest and Civil Disorder Impacting Market Stability**  
Escalations in social and ethnic tensions, such as protests and riot-triggering events seen in the U.S. and other regions, add layers of operational risk for Merck. Events like the 2017 Charlottesville rally and COVID-19-related protests in China (2022) create pockets of instability that could disrupt local operations, manufacturing sites, or market access temporarily. Such unrest increases the risk of damage to infrastructure or logistical delays and may lead to changes in public healthcare policy priorities or consumer behavior, indirectly affecting Merck’s sales and marketing effectiveness.

**5. Sector-Wide Exposure to Disruptive Technologies and Industry Shifts**  
Although Merck has been influenced by transformative technological developments (e.g., linked to consumer tech milestones and emerging AI trends since 2006), these developments also expose the pharmaceutical landscape to disruption from innovative competitors and shifting R&D paradigms. The sector’s gradual digitalization and AI adoption raise the bar for innovation speed and cost efficiency. Failure to adapt quickly could erode competitive positioning and margin stability, increasing credit risk over the medium term as industry dynamics evolve.

---

Overall, the operating environment for Merck & Co., Inc. is increasingly characterized by complex geopolitical risks, regulatory unpredictability, recurrent health crises, and social unrest. These factors combine to elevate the company’s exposure to revenue volatility, operational disruptions, and elevated compliance costs, thereby imposing downward pressure on its credit risk profile despite the inherent demand stability in the pharmaceutical sector.
# V3 corrected

### Key Rating Drivers – Operating Environment (F3)

**1. Geopolitical Instability in Eastern Europe**  
The escalation of the Russo-Ukrainian War in 2022 has created significant uncertainty for Merck & Co., Inc., particularly in its operations and supply chains within Europe and surrounding regions. The conflict has disrupted logistics and increased risks related to trade restrictions, sanctions, and economic instability. Continued or expanded hostilities could further complicate market access and regulatory processes, impacting revenue stability and operating costs in affected geographies.

**2. Regulatory and Policy Uncertainty in Major Healthcare Markets**  
Frequent shifts in U.S. political leadership and healthcare policy have contributed to a volatile regulatory landscape. The transitions between presidential administrations from 2016 onward have brought varying approaches to drug pricing, patent protections, and pharmaceutical regulations. This unpredictability increases the risk of adverse legislative or regulatory actions that could pressure product pricing, reimbursement levels, and market exclusivity, challenging Merck’s financial forecasting and strategic planning.

**3. Exposure to Health Crises and Pandemic Risks**  
Recurring global health emergencies such as the SARS outbreak (2002-2004), the COVID-19 pandemic, and the 2022 monkeypox outbreak emphasize Merck’s exposure to pandemic-related operational disruptions. While these events can increase demand for certain therapeutic products, they also create production and supply challenges, regulatory uncertainties, and heightened competition in critical vaccine and antiviral segments. The unpredictable duration and scale of pandemics complicate production planning and may strain R&D resources, affecting overall business continuity.

**4. Social Unrest and Market Disruptions**  
Instances of social unrest and protests, including events related to ethnic and political tensions in the U.S. and COVID-19 protests in China in 2022, pose localized operational risks. Such disturbances can affect distribution channels, manufacturing facilities, and workforce availability in key markets. Disruptions driven by social or political unrest add complexity to maintaining stable operations and could lead to temporary revenue fluctuations or increased security and compliance costs.

---

Merck’s operating environment is shaped by tangible geopolitical risks, regulatory unpredictability, ongoing pandemic challenges, and episodic social unrest. These external factors present consistent challenges to stable operations and credit risk, warranting close monitoring despite the company’s solid sector positioning.
# FINAL
---------------------------------------------------
# Merck & Co., Inc.  
## Credit Risk Report – 2022  
### Key Rating Drivers  

This section outlines the primary factors influencing Merck & Co., Inc.’s credit risk profile for the year 2022. While the company demonstrates robust absolute cash flow generation and strong liquidity, several internal and external risks warrant attention. The challenges largely stem from margin pressures, elevated leverage, structural business complexities, and an uncertain operating environment shaped by geopolitical and regulatory volatility. The following analysis, segmented by financial profile, business profile, and operating environment factors, provides a detailed view of these credit risk drivers.  

---

## Financial Profile (F1)  

### Pressure on Profitability Margins Limits Debt Servicing Flexibility  
Merck’s EBITDA margin declined modestly to 36.0% in 2022 from 36.8% in 2021, indicating growing cost pressures or weakening pricing power. This margin compression diminishes the company’s earnings cushion and its ability to absorb financial shocks. Compared with peers like AbbVie, which posted EBITDA margins exceeding 41%, Merck’s relatively constrained profitability reduces its buffer against unforeseen operational challenges. This weakening earnings capacity can restrict debt servicing flexibility, increasing credit risk if adverse conditions intensify.  

### Leveraging Trend Reflects Rising Financial Risk Amid Modest Cash Flow Growth  
Despite an improvement in leverage ratios—EBITDA leverage dropping from 1.85x to 1.44x and FFO leverage from 1.96x to 1.32x—the company maintains a moderately elevated debt burden relative to cash flow. This less conservative capital structure reduces financial flexibility, particularly if future cash flows soften or if capital investment demands increase. The risk associated with this leveraging trend is underscored by earnings volatility, which could exacerbate challenges in maintaining stable leverage metrics and CFO confidence.  

### Capital Expenditure Intensity Moderates Free Cash Flow Conversion and Discretionary Capacity  
Merck’s sizeable capital expenditures (~$4.4 billion annually) limit free cash flow (FCF) conversion to less than 50% of EBITDA, constraining discretionary cash generation. Although operating cash flow and absolute FCF ($14.7 billion in 2022) remain strong, ongoing investments impose a ceiling on available resources to accelerate debt reduction, fund strategic initiatives, or build liquidity buffers. The moderate free cash flow conversion rate highlights potential cash quality issues should CapEx requirements increase or working capital pressures mount.  

### Interest Coverage Adequate but Vulnerable to Earnings Volatility  
Funds from operations to interest expense improved to 3.3x in 2022, reflecting comfortable coverage at current earnings levels. However, Merck’s profitability stress and leverage position make this ratio sensitive to downturns in operating performance. A moderate decline in cash flow due to patent expiries, competitive pressures, or regulatory changes could materially weaken interest coverage ratios, increasing credit risk and potentially pressuring debt servicing capacity.  

### Strong Near-Term Liquidity Yet Limited Cushion Against Market Stress  
Merck’s cash balance relative to short-term debt surged strongly to approximately 6.5x, substantially exceeding peer baselines and significantly reducing near-term refinancing risk. Nevertheless, the company’s free cash flow ratio remaining below 0.5 underscores limited internal liquidity generation beyond capital expenditures. This dynamic implies that while short-term liquidity is robust, sustained operational disruptions or tightening external financing conditions could strain financial flexibility.  

---

## Business Profile (F2)  

### Elevated Insider Equity Awards Pose Shareholder Dilution Risk  
In 2022, Merck continued a high volume of phantom stock and common stock awards to insiders. While designed to retain key talent, this practice increases the risk of shareholder dilution, potentially reducing earnings per share and shareholder value over time. Such dilution can intensify pressure on financial performance metrics and weigh on credit quality, especially absent commensurate improvements in operational or financial outcomes.  

### Dispersed Insider Ownership Limits Strategic Cohesion and Control  
The company’s insider ownership remains broadly dispersed without significant concentration. This structure reduces risk of control conflicts but may also weaken alignment between management and shareholders, potentially impeding agile and decisive strategic initiatives. In a highly competitive and regulated pharmaceutical landscape, this dispersed control could complicate efforts to swiftly navigate emerging challenges or capitalize on market opportunities, perpetuating strategic inconsistency.  

### Minority Stakes in Multiple Biopharma Subsidiaries Increase Management Complexity and Indirect Risk Exposure  
Merck’s partial ownership (~10%) in various biopharma subsidiaries, such as Organon & Co. and BeOne Medicines Ltd., limits its ability to influence these entities meaningfully. Managing diverse minority stakes multiplies operational complexity, dispersing managerial focus and resources. Additionally, Merck remains exposed to the financial and governance risks inherent to these subsidiaries without full control, which could materialize as indirect credit pressure if any subsidiary underperforms or faces capital shortfalls.  

### Insider Transaction Patterns Reflect Cautious Confidence without Strong Bullish Signals  
Insider transactions in 2022 skewed toward stock awards and modest purchases, with negligible disposals. This pattern suggests a stable but cautious insider outlook amid external uncertainties but lacks the conviction signaling robust near-term growth expectations. The cautious stance could be interpreted as management’s prudent conservatism facing an uneven operating landscape, which signals moderate business risk from potential strategic inertia.  

---

## Operating Environment (F3)  

### Geopolitical Instability in Eastern Europe Adds Operational and Supply Chain Risks  
The ongoing Russo-Ukrainian war continues to inject significant uncertainty into Merck’s European operations and supply chains. Disruptions in logistics, trade restrictions, and sanctions pose tangible risks to market access and regulatory compliance. Escalations or prolonged conflict heighten the likelihood of revenue volatility and increased operating costs in affected markets, undermining financial predictability.  

### Regulatory and Policy Volatility in Major Healthcare Markets Threatens Revenue Stability  
In the United States, shifting political landscapes contribute to regulatory unpredictability affecting drug pricing, patent protections, and reimbursement policies. This environment imposes considerable challenges on Merck’s ability to forecast revenues and plan long-term investments with confidence. Heightened risk of adverse legislative or executive actions can pressure margins and market exclusivity, constraining pricing power and eroding competitive positioning.  

### Exposure to Pandemic-Related Operational Disruptions and Competitive Pressures  
Although global health crises can temporarily enhance demand for certain products, recurring pandemics—evidenced by COVID-19 and the 2022 monkeypox outbreak—pose multifaceted challenges. These include manufacturing and supply constraints, regulatory approval delays, and intensified competition for vaccines and antivirals. The unpredictable nature of pandemics complicates resource allocation, strains R&D budgets, and amplifies operational volatility, increasing credit risk through earnings uncertainty.  

### Episodic Social Unrest and Market Disruptions Increase Operational Complexity  
Localized social unrest related to ethnic, political, or public health tensions—such as those seen in the U.S. and China during 2022—introduce risks of workforce disruptions, supply chain interruptions, and elevated compliance costs. Though episodic, these disturbances contribute to an uneven operating environment, potentially leading to revenue fluctuations and increased security expenditures.  

---

## Conclusion  

Merck & Co., Inc.’s 2022 credit risk profile is shaped by a mix of solid absolute cash flow generation and a strong near-term liquidity position, tempered by notable challenges on both internal and external fronts. The company faces pressures from compressing profitability margins, a moderately leveraged capital structure, and substantial ongoing capital expenditure demands that limit free cash flow conversion. Its business profile reflects risks of shareholder dilution from frequent equity awards, dispersed insider ownership potentially diluting strategic focus, and complex exposures tied to multiple minority subsidiary stakes. Externally, a challenging operating environment dominated by geopolitical tensions in Eastern Europe, regulatory uncertainty in key markets, pandemic-related operational disruptions, and episodic social unrest further heighten credit risk.  

Collectively, these factors restrict Merck’s financial and strategic flexibility and introduce potential volatility in earnings and cash flows, necessitating vigilant monitoring to safeguard credit quality over the medium term.
# FINAL - with sample
---------------------------------------------------
### Key Rating Drivers – Merck & Co., Inc. (2022)

This section outlines the principal factors influencing Merck’s credit risk profile for 2022, emphasizing key vulnerabilities and risks across the financial profile, business profile, and operating environment. While Merck displays robust operating cash flow and a leading-market presence, the rating drivers predominantly reflect pressures on profitability, moderate financial leverage, internal strategic complexities, and challenging external conditions that collectively constrain the company’s financial flexibility and credit standing.

---

## Financial Profile (F1)

### Pressure on Profitability Margins Limits Earnings Cushion and Debt Servicing Flexibility  
Merck’s EBITDA margin contracted modestly to 36.0% in 2022 from 36.8% in 2021, signaling marginal margin compression amid cost pressures or constrained pricing power. This decline reduces the company’s earnings buffer to absorb operational shocks or fund debt obligations, particularly when benchmarked against peers like AbbVie, which reported EBITDA margins above 41% during the same period. The relative lower margin profile indicates competitive challenges that may impair sustained profitability and heighten sensitivity to adverse market or regulatory developments.

### Elevated Leverage and Moderate Cash Flow Growth Indicate Rising Financial Risk  
Although leverage ratios improved—EBITDA leverage declined from 1.85x to 1.44x and FFO leverage from 1.96x to 1.32x—these remain elevated relative to Merck’s longer-term historical levels. The moderate deleveraging stems primarily from incremental EBITDA and funds from operations growth rather than material debt reduction, reflecting a financial structure that is less conservative. This increased leverage profile reduces financial flexibility, leaving Merck more vulnerable should operating cash flows weaken due to competitive pressures, product lifecycle declines, or unforeseen capital demands.

### Significant Capital Expenditure Demands Constrain Free Cash Flow Conversion and Discretionary Resources  
Despite strong absolute free cash flow (approximately $14.7 billion), Merck’s conversion of EBITDA into free cash flow stands below 50%, mainly due to substantial capital expenditures of about $4.4 billion annually. This high reinvestment rate structurally limits the availability of discretionary cash flow for accelerated debt repayment or liquidity enhancement. Additionally, the sizable CapEx burden, compounded by potential working capital fluctuations, constrains the company’s capacity to bolster its balance sheet without increasing leverage or curtailing strategic investments.

### Interest Coverage Adequate but Vulnerable to Earnings Volatility  
Merck achieved a funds from operations to interest expense ratio of 3.3x in 2022, suggesting currently comfortable interest coverage. Nevertheless, the ratio remains susceptible to deterioration should earnings fall, given the company’s moderate leverage and margin pressures. Earnings volatility driven by product patent expirations, pricing pressures, or regulatory hurdles could materially erode coverage, intensifying credit risk and potentially increasing borrowing costs or refinancing challenges.

### Strong Near-Term Liquidity Provides Cushion but Free Cash Flow Efficiency Remains Subdued  
The company maintains a substantial liquidity buffer, with cash to short-term debt ratio rising to roughly 6.5x in 2022, significantly reducing immediate refinancing risk. However, despite this liquidity strength, Merck’s free cash flow ratio (free cash flow to EBITDA) remains under 0.5, signaling that large portions of earnings are reinvested or tied up in working capital. This moderate cash quality limits flexibility in adverse scenarios where external funding markets could tighten or operational cash flows decline.

---

## Business Profile (F2)

### Frequent and Significant Insider Equity Awards Pose Dilution and Financial Pressure Risks  
Merck’s continuous issuance of phantom stock and common stock awards to executives and insiders represents a persistent shareholder dilution risk. While equity-based compensation aligns with pharmaceutical industry practices for talent retention, the volume and frequency of equity awards could suppress earnings per share and shareholder returns over time. Dilution pressure complicates capital structure management and may constrain credit metrics if not balanced by commensurate increases in shareholder value or performance improvements.

### Dispersed Insider Ownership Limits Strategic Cohesion and Swift Decision-Making  
In the absence of concentrated insider ownership, Merck experiences a dispersed control environment among its executives. This governance structure, while mitigating risks of activist interference, may also hamper rapid strategic responses and dilute executive incentives for alignment with shareholder interests. Such fragmentation can hinder decisive management action, an important consideration given the dynamic and highly regulated nature of the pharmaceutical industry.

### Operational Complexity and Limited Control from Minority Stakes in Multiple Biopharma Subsidiaries  
Merck’s partial ownership—approximately 10% stakes—in diverse smaller biopharma subsidiaries such as Organon & Co., BeOne Medicines Ltd., and VYNE Therapeutics increases strategic and operational complexity. The limited influence over these entities restricts Merck’s ability to fully integrate or guide their development, while also exposing the parent company to indirect risks related to governance issues, capital needs, or performance shortfalls. Managing multiple minorities detracts focus and may dilute resources that could otherwise support core operations or deleveraging.

### Insider Transaction Patterns Reflect Moderate Confidence with Limited Strong Accumulation  
Insider transactional behavior during 2022 was generally balanced between stock awards and minor purchases, with few significant sales. This pattern denotes stability in insider sentiment but lacks compelling evidence of heightened confidence in near-term growth prospects. The restrained transactions underscore caution amid external uncertainties rather than exuberant endorsement of the company’s outlook, implying moderate strategic and operational risks.

---

## Operating Environment (F3)

### Ongoing Geopolitical Instability in Eastern Europe Disrupts Supply Chains and Market Access  
The intensification of the Russo-Ukrainian conflict during 2022 has materially elevated risks to Merck’s European operations. Disruptions include logistical challenges, heightened tariffs, trade restrictions, and broader economic instability, all of which undermine consistent market access and elevate operating costs. Prolonged or escalated hostilities threaten to further destabilize supply chains and complicate regulatory compliance across affected territories, adversely impacting revenue stability.

### Heightened Regulatory and Policy Uncertainty in Key Healthcare Markets Threatens Pricing and Reimbursement  
Frequent changes in healthcare policies, particularly in the U.S. under shifting presidential administrations, create an unpredictable regulatory backdrop. Variability in drug pricing reforms, patent law adjustments, and pharmaceutical market regulations heightens uncertainty regarding reimbursement levels and exclusivity periods. This environment challenges Merck’s ability to accurately forecast revenues and maintain stable profit margins, adding complexity to R&D prioritization and long-term strategic planning.

### Recurring Pandemic and Health Crisis Risks Complicate Production and Supply Planning  
Merck’s exposure to persistent global health emergencies—highlighted by COVID-19 and the 2022 monkeypox outbreak—illustrates vulnerability to unpredictable demand spikes, regulatory shifts, and supply chain disruptions. While pandemic conditions can increase sales of select therapeutics and vaccines, they also strain manufacturing capacity, delay ongoing R&D projects, and intensify competition from other pharmaceutical and biotech companies. These factors exacerbate operational volatility and complicate resource allocation.

### Social Unrest and Political Tensions Create Localized Operational Risks and Cost Pressures  
Episodes of social unrest across key markets, including the U.S. and China, have intermittently disrupted distribution chains, manufacturing sites, and workforce availability. These disturbances add operational uncertainty and may lead to temporary revenue dips or heightened security and compliance expenditures. In a globally integrated business, such regional disruptions cumulatively increase operational risk and cost volatility.

---

### Conclusion

Merck & Co., Inc.’s credit risk position in 2022 is shaped primarily by moderate but persistent headwinds across its financial and business profiles, and a challenging operating environment. Earnings and margin pressures, elevated leverage despite moderate deleveraging, and constrained free cash flow conversion limit financial flexibility. Concurrently, governance complexities from dispersed insider ownership, strategic dilution from equity compensation, and operational encumbrances from diversified minority stakes serve as internal risk multipliers. Externally, geopolitical tensions, regulatory unpredictability, pandemic-related uncertainties, and episodic social unrest compound operational challenges. Collectively, these factors warrant careful ongoing assessment as they elevate Merck’s vulnerability to downside credit events, despite a generally strong liquidity position and solid sector placement.
# V0

### Key Rating Drivers for Merck & Co., Inc. (CIK 0000310158) – 2025

---

#### 1. **Pressure on Profit Margins Due to Increased R&D and Commercial Expenses (F1 – Financial Profile)**  
Merck continues to face significant margin compression driven by heightened investment in research and development (R&D) as it attempts to replenish its drug pipeline amid patent expirations on key products. The company’s elevated R&D spending, coupled with increased commercial expenses to support product launches and market expansions, constrains operating leverage and profitability. While these investments are critical for future growth, they exert meaningful pressure on free cash flow generation and limit near-term financial flexibility.

---

#### 2. **Exposure to Patent Cliffs and Generic Competition (F2 – Business Profile)**  
Merck’s revenue profile remains vulnerable to patent expirations on several legacy blockbuster drugs, which have invited aggressive generic competition. This dynamic undermines the company’s pricing power and revenue predictability, increasing earnings volatility. Despite efforts to bolster the portfolio through new product launches and acquisitions, the timeline for meaningful contributions from these initiatives remains uncertain, heightening business risk in the medium term.

---

#### 3. **High Leverage Levels and Limited Financial Flexibility (F1 – Financial Profile)**  
The company’s elevated debt load, incurred partly to finance recent acquisitions and shareholder returns, results in a leverage ratio that restricts financial flexibility. Although Merck generates substantial cash flows, the combination of high R&D spending and significant interest expenses reduces headroom for debt servicing under stressed conditions. This constrained liquidity profile increases vulnerability to adverse business or market developments.

---

#### 4. **Regulatory and Pricing Pressures in the Pharmaceutical Industry (F3 – Operating Environment)**  
Merck operates in a highly regulated sector subject to increasing scrutiny over drug pricing and reimbursement policies, particularly in the United States. Ongoing political pressure for healthcare cost containment introduces uncertainty regarding future pricing frameworks, potentially eroding revenue and profitability. Regulatory hurdles for product approvals and market access delays also add complexity to the company’s growth outlook.

---

#### 5. **Competitive Intensity and Innovation Risk (F2 – Business Profile)**  
The pharmaceutical industry’s competitive landscape is characterized by rapid innovation and the presence of formidable peers with deep R&D capabilities. Merck faces substantial risk that its development efforts may not yield commercially successful products in a timely manner, exposing the company to revenue shortfalls. Strategic decisions around pipeline prioritization and resource allocation remain critical, given the high failure rates associated with drug development.

---

#### 6. **Exposure to Macroeconomic Volatility and Geopolitical Risks (F3 – Operating Environment)**  
Merck’s global operations expose it to macroeconomic fluctuations, including inflationary pressures that may increase costs, and currency exchange volatility impacting international revenue streams. Additionally, geopolitical tensions and trade disruptions – especially in emerging markets that represent growth opportunities – could affect supply chains and market access, further complicating business execution and financial predictability.

---

### Summary  
Merck’s credit profile in 2025 is challenged by its high R&D and commercial spending amid revenue pressures from patent expirations and industry-wide pricing constraints. Elevated leverage limits financial flexibility, while regulatory, competitive, and macroeconomic uncertainties add layers of risk to the company’s operational and financial stability. These factors collectively weigh on the credit risk assessment despite Merck’s strong market presence and robust cash flow generation potential.

### Key Rating Drivers – Financial Profile (F1)

#### Volatility and Decline in Profitability Margins
Merck & Co., Inc. has exhibited significant fluctuations in its EBITDA margin over the recent three-year period, with margin compression notably evident in 2023 where it dropped sharply to approximately 11.5% from over 35% in 2022 and 40% in 2024. This volatility raises concerns regarding the company’s sustained operational profitability and its ability to consistently generate earnings sufficient to service and repay debt. Compared to peers like AbbVie, which maintains a more stable EBITDA margin closer to 26-42%, Merck’s margin instability may reflect challenges in cost management, pricing pressures, or product portfolio transitions, undermining predictability in profit generation.

#### Leverage Levels and Sustainability Risks
While Merck’s EBITDA and fund from operations (FFO) leverage ratios have improved from the heightened levels seen in 2023, leverage remains moderately elevated with EBITDA leverage near 1.44x and FFO leverage around 1.45x as of 2024. Although these ratios suggest moderate indebtedness relative to cash flow, the rapid swings in leverage—particularly the notable spike in EBITDA leverage exceeding 5x in 2023—highlight susceptibility to earnings volatility. Sustained leverage at this level amid profitability uncertainty could pressure credit metrics, especially if cash flow deteriorates in an adverse scenario.

#### Interest Coverage Cushion Is Adequate but Sensitive to Earnings Volatility
Current coverage metrics, indicated by an FFO interest coverage ratio above 3.2x in 2024, offer a comfortable buffer for debt service under baseline conditions. However, given Merck’s earnings and cash flow volatility, coverage ratios could deteriorate materially under a 10-20% decrease in FFO or cash flow from operations (CFO), potentially compressing the ratio below prudent thresholds. This vulnerability imposes risk under credit stress or economic downturns that might reduce operational earnings.

#### Cash Flow Generation Quality and Conversion Efficiency Concerns
Although Merck delivers strong absolute cash flow figures across FFO, CFO, and free cash flow (FCF), inspection of their relative growth and conversion rates reveals fluctuations in cash conversion efficiency. Particularly in 2023, the divergence between relatively weaker EBITDA and stronger CFO and FCF points to potential variability in working capital or one-off cash items, which could impair the predictability and quality of cash flows available for debt servicing. Moreover, the free cash flow ratio, although improving in 2024 to nearly 0.49, remains volatile, signaling potential pressure on liquidity if CapEx or operational cash demand suddenly rises.

#### Liquidity and Short-Term Refinancing Risks
Despite holding a robust cash position exceeding $13 billion in 2024 and maintaining a strong cash-to-short-term debt ratio near 5x, the company’s near-term liquidity is somewhat exposed to cash flow volatility. The free cash flow ratio under 0.5 indicates a limited cushion after operational and investment outflows, which, combined with episodic leverage elevation, could exacerbate refinancing risks if markets tighten or capital access becomes constrained. This is accentuated by the notable decrease in cash balances relative to short-term debt observed in previous years.

#### Relative Weakness Versus Sector Peers
In contrast to peers such as AbbVie, Merck’s financial profile demonstrates more pronounced earnings volatility, less stable margins, and comparatively tighter liquidity ratios. AbbVie’s more consistent EBITDA margins and higher interest coverage ratios underline stronger operational resilience and financial flexibility within the sector. Merck’s episodic leverage spikes and margin compression create a less stable credit profile relative to such benchmarks, signaling greater downside risk under stress conditions.

#### Projection of Financial Stability and Credit Risk
Looking forward, Merck’s financial profile suggests a risk profile that remains susceptible to earnings fluctuations and operational cycle pressures. While recent improvements in leverage and coverage metrics provide some relief, underlying volatility in profitability and cash flow conversion efficiency warrants caution. Potential margin pressures, coupled with capital expenditure demands, could tighten financial flexibility and increase refinancing risks, particularly in adverse macroeconomic or sector-specific environments. Consequently, credit risk remains moderately elevated with the need for prudent liquidity management and margin stabilization to maintain rating stability.
# V1 corrected
---------------------------------------------------
### Key Rating Drivers – Financial Profile (F1)

#### Significant Volatility in EBITDA Margins and Profitability
Merck & Co., Inc. has experienced considerable fluctuations in its EBITDA margin over the past three years, with a steep decline to approximately 11.5% in 2023 before rebounding strongly to about 40% in 2024. This sharp margin variability highlights inconsistent profitability, which increases uncertainty around the company’s ability to generate stable earnings to support debt service and operational needs. Such margin swings may stem from irregular revenue streams, changing product dynamics, or fluctuating cost structures. Compared to sector peer AbbVie, which has maintained more consistent margins between 26% and 42%, Merck’s earnings volatility represents a credit risk factor.

#### Fluctuating and Elevated Leverage Levels
Merck’s leverage ratios have shown pronounced volatility, with EBITDA leverage reaching over 5.0x in 2023 before sharply improving to approximately 1.44x in 2024. Although leverage levels normalized recently, the prior period’s spike reflects susceptibility to earnings weakness and potential financial strain under adverse conditions. Sustaining leverage near 1.4x in 2024 indicates moderate indebtedness relative to its cash generation; however, the historical variability points to material risk if EBITDA or cash flow deteriorates again. The FFO leverage ratio shows a similar trend, underscoring the importance of stable cash flows to maintain credit metrics.

#### Interest Coverage Margins While Adequate, Sensitive to Earnings Volatility
Interest coverage based on funds from operations remains healthy, with a coverage ratio of approximately 3.27x in 2024, indicating currently sufficient capacity to meet interest expenses. Nonetheless, coverage levels are sensitive to earnings fluctuations; any 10-20% decline in FFO or CFO could compress this ratio, reducing debt service flexibility. Given the company’s earnings and cash flow volatility, this sensitivity heightens financial risk in a downturn scenario or if operational challenges arise.

#### Variability in Cash Flow Conversion and Free Cash Flow Consistency
Although Merck consistently generates substantial FFO, CFO, and free cash flow, the conversion of EBITDA to operating cash flow has varied over the three years, particularly illustrated by the sharp drop in EBITDA margin and corresponding cash metrics in 2023. Differences between EBITDA and CFO/FCF growth rates suggest possible inconsistencies in working capital management or non-recurring cash flow items, which could impair the predictability and quality of cash generation. The free cash flow ratio improved significantly to nearly 0.49 in 2024, but its volatility constrains reliable forecasting and liquidity planning.

#### Liquidity Profile Generally Strong but With Limited Near-Term Cushion
The company’s cash position, at approximately $13.2 billion in 2024, combined with a high cash-to-short-term debt ratio near 5.0x, reflects solid liquidity to cover immediate obligations and short-term maturities. However, the free cash flow ratio under 0.5 points to a moderate buffer after capital expenditures and operating cash requirements. This limited free cash flow cushion, coupled with prior leverage spikes, indicates potential vulnerability if cash flow generation weakens or if refinancing conditions tighten in the near term.

#### Weaker Financial Stability Compared to Sector Peer
Compared with AbbVie, Merck exhibits greater earnings and cash flow volatility, less consistent margin performance, and tighter free cash flow ratios. AbbVie’s generally stable EBITDA margins and higher liquidity flexibility underscore more resilient financial metrics in the sector. Merck’s fluctuations in leverage, cash conversion efficiency, and margin stability present heightened credit risk relative to its peer, necessitating closer monitoring of cash flow quality and earnings consistency.

#### Elevated Exposure to Earnings and Cash Flow Volatility
The pronounced swings in profitability and leverage over a short time horizon expose Merck to elevated credit risk, especially under less favorable market or business conditions. This earnings volatility could adversely impact leverage multiples and coverage metrics, shrinking financial flexibility and increasing refinancing risk. Maintaining stable margins and improving cash flow quality will be critical to mitigating these risks and sustaining credit profile robustness.

#### CapEx Levels and Their Impact on Free Cash Flow Generation
Capital expenditures have remained fairly stable in absolute terms, ranging from approximately $3.4 billion to $4.4 billion annually. While this reflects consistent investment in the business, the relatively high and steady CapEx level limits the growth of free cash flow, especially in years of compressed EBITDA margin. This dynamic reduces the flexibility to boost discretionary cash flow or reduce indebtedness rapidly in periods of operational stress.

---

Overall, Merck & Co., Inc.’s financial profile demonstrates moderate but fluctuating leverage and varying cash flow quality. The company’s credit risk is heightened by earnings and margin volatility, uneven cash flow conversion, and a limited free cash flow cushion after capital investments. Though liquidity remains sound, consistent profitability and stronger cash flow generation will be required to enhance financial stability and mitigate refinancing and earnings risk going forward.

#### OWNER TABLE

### Key Rating Drivers – Business Profile (F2)

**Elevated Insider Sales Activity Indicating Potential Concerns on Strategic Outlook**  
Throughout 2025, Merck & Co., Inc. has experienced a relatively high volume of insider sales transactions, predominantly through awards and stock disposals by several key executives and senior management including names such as Mary Ellen Coe and David Michael Williams. The frequency and volume of these transactions—spanning both phantom stock and common stock—suggest a notable shift in insider behavior compared to historical patterns. While some insider sales may be routine or related to diversification, the clustering of significant disposals within the first three quarters of 2025 could imply reduced long-term confidence in the company’s near-term strategic outlook or an expectation of limited upside in equity value. This elevated activity raises questions about alignment between management incentives and shareholder interests, potentially influencing credit risk perceptions.

**Lack of Ownership Consolidation and Potential Dilution from Stock Incentive Programs**  
Merck’s equity ownership remains broadly dispersed, with no indication of meaningful concentration among insiders or core shareholders. The continued issuance of phantom stock and stock awards as a part of compensation packages raises concerns over dilution risk for existing shareholders. Given the steady pace of awards being transacted, the company may be relying substantially on equity incentives to retain and motivate talent, which—if unchecked—could erode shareholder value and dilute earnings per share, thereby impacting financial flexibility over time. This could weigh negatively on credit quality if it restricts the company’s ability to preserve strong capital reserves or reinvest earnings efficiently.

**Complex and Extensive Subsidiary Ownership Increasing Organizational Complexity and Operational Risk**  
Merck & Co., Inc. maintains part ownership (approximately 10%) in a diverse portfolio of biotech and pharmaceutical entities including BeOne Medicines Ltd., Harpoon Therapeutics, Inc., Organon & Co., and others. While this diversified footprint supports innovation pipelines, the partial ownership stakes limit Merck’s direct control and influence over critical subsidiaries. This fragmented group structure may expose Merck to heightened operational and strategic risk, as alignment with minority-owned entities can be challenging, potentially limiting synergies and the company’s ability to swiftly redirect resources or business strategies. Moreover, partial ownership stakes, especially in nascent biotech firms, increase exposure to high-risk ventures with uncertain cash flow profiles, which could strain group-level resources if substantial capital support or restructuring becomes necessary.

**Absence of Insider Purchases Amid Elevated Market Uncertainties**  
The insider transaction patterns show a distinct lack of material insider purchases during 2025 and a dominance of awards and sales over acquisitions. The absence of insider buying activity at a senior management level, particularly in a sector as volatile and innovation-dependent as pharmaceuticals, raises questions about confidence in near-term growth prospects or management’s assessment of valuation adequacy. In credit risk terms, limited insider buying removes an important positive signaling tool and suggests exposure to downside risks from unanticipated regulatory, competitive, or pipeline development challenges.

---

These factors collectively underscore growing uncertainty over strategic execution and stakeholder alignment within Merck’s business profile, highlighting potential vulnerabilities that could impinge on the company’s long-term credit standing despite its broadly resilient industry position.
# V2 corrected
---------------------------------------------------
### Key Rating Drivers – Business Profile (F2)

**Elevated Insider Stock Awards Increasing Potential Dilution Risk**  
In 2025, Merck & Co., Inc. continued to grant a significant number of phantom stock awards to executives and insiders. The volume of these equity-based compensation awards indicates ongoing reliance on stock incentives to retain and motivate key personnel. While this is typical in the pharmaceutical industry, the steady issuance of such awards contributes to potential dilution of existing shareholders’ equity. Over time, this dilution risk may weigh on earnings per share and investor returns, potentially impacting investor confidence and financial flexibility.

**Insider Transactions Concentrated in Equity Awards Rather Than Purchases**  
The insider transaction activity for 2025 is dominated by awards of phantom stock rather than insider purchases of company stock. The absence of insider buying signals a cautious outlook from key executives and insiders regarding the stock’s near-term upside. This pattern may suggest limited internal conviction in current valuation levels or growth prospects, reducing positive alignment between management and shareholders’ interests.

**Complex Group Structure with Minority Stakes in Multiple Biotech and Pharma Entities**  
Merck maintains numerous minority ownership stakes (approximately 10%) across a broad range of biotech subsidiaries and affiliates, such as Organon & Co., Harpoon Therapeutics, Inc., and BeOne Medicines Ltd. While these stakes provide access to innovative pipelines, the limited ownership percentage restricts Merck’s operational control and influence. This structure introduces organizational complexity and potential governance challenges, as strategic alignment and decision-making with partially owned entities is more difficult. The exposure to multiple smaller ventures also heightens the risk profile due to potentially uneven financial and operational performance across these affiliates.

**Lack of Insider Buying Activity Limits Confidence Signals to Investors**  
During 2025, insider stock acquisitions were negligible. This absence of insider purchases removes a typical positive indicator of management’s confidence in the company’s outlook. In an industry characterized by regulatory uncertainties and pipeline development risks, insider buying serves as a valuable signal of conviction; its lack contributes to increased perceived risk regarding the business strategy’s execution and long-term growth potential.

These business profile factors, including reliance on equity awards, dispersed insider ownership, limited control over affiliated ventures, and muted insider confidence signals, collectively add to the risk considerations in Merck’s credit profile by highlighting potential vulnerabilities in management incentive alignment and operational complexity.
# V3
---------------------------------------------------
### Key Rating Drivers – Operating Environment (F3)

#### Heightened Geopolitical Risks and Global Conflicts  
Merck & Co., Inc.’s operating environment faces significant challenges from ongoing geopolitical tensions and armed conflicts, notably the Russo-Ukrainian War which escalated in 2022. The invasion has disrupted global supply chains, increased raw material costs, and introduced uncertainty in international markets. Additionally, the company’s exposure to political instability related to protests and civil unrest—such as the Euromaidan uprising in Ukraine and escalating ethnic and religious tensions in the United States—may result in operational interruptions and heightened security costs. These geopolitical risks complicate forecasting and may pressure margins given the unpredictability of trade and regulatory environments.

#### Increased Regulatory and Political Uncertainty Linked to Power Transfers  
Frequent and significant political transitions in key markets have introduced a layer of uncertainty affecting Merck’s strategic planning and compliance costs. U.S. presidential elections, including the contested 2020 election and subsequent power transfers, have led to fluctuating healthcare policies and drug pricing reforms that could negatively affect profitability. Furthermore, international political events, such as Brexit, continue to reshape trade agreements and regulatory frameworks, potentially increasing barriers and operational costs. The dynamic political landscape introduces risks of sudden legislative changes detrimental to the company’s market access and reimbursement environment.

#### Vulnerability to Health Crises and Pandemics  
Merck’s operations remain exposed to the disruptive effects of health crises, demonstrated by its experience during the COVID-19 pandemic and subsequent outbreaks such as monkeypox. These pandemics have caused widespread supply chain disruptions, increased demand volatility, and intensified regulatory scrutiny. The social instability triggered by health-related protests, notably in China during the COVID-19 pandemic, further impacts workforce availability and distribution channels. The persistence or recurrence of such crises could impair production continuity, delay clinical trials, and strain financial performance.

#### Escalation of Protests and Social Unrest Impacting Operational Stability  
The company’s global footprint exposes it to risks associated with increasing frequency and severity of protests and civil disturbances. Events like the Charlottesville rally in the U.S. and protests triggered by pandemic restrictions in various regions place operational sites at risk of disruption. Social unrest can not only affect manufacturing and distribution but also create reputational risks amid heightened public scrutiny. These factors create an unfavorable operating backdrop, potentially increasing operational costs and complicating stakeholder relations.

#### Exposure to Disruptive Technological Advances in the Sector  
The pharmaceutical industry is increasingly influenced by rapid technological evolutions, particularly in digital health and artificial intelligence. While Merck has engaged with some consumer technology milestones, the acceleration of disruptive technologies by competitors poses risks to traditional business models. The sector-wide adoption of innovations demands significant investment and agility; failure to keep pace could erode competitive positioning. This technological disruption in the external environment introduces uncertainty regarding future revenue streams and capital allocation.

---

Overall, these operating environment factors combine to elevate Merck & Co., Inc.’s credit risk profile through increased volatility in regulatory, geopolitical, and health-related domains. The company must navigate a complex landscape shaped by global conflicts, political shifts, public health challenges, social instability, and rapid technological change, all of which could adversely impact financial and operational performance in 2025.
